### Municipalities

Main article: [List of municipalities in Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_Colorado)

**Map this section's coordinates in "List of municipalities in Colorado" using** [OpenStreetMap](https://tools.wmflabs.org/osm4wiki/cgi-bin/wiki/wiki-osm.pl?project=en\&article=List+of+municipalities+in+Colorado\&section=Municipalities)&#x20;

**Download coordinates as:**

- [KML](https://tools.wmflabs.org/kmlexport?article=List+of+municipalities+in+Colorado\&section=Municipalities)

- [GPX (all coordinates)](https://geoexport.toolforge.org/gpx?project=en.wikipedia.org\&coprimary=all\&titles=List+of+municipalities+in+Colorado\&section=Municipalities)

- [GPX (primary coordinates)](https://geoexport.toolforge.org/gpx?project=en.wikipedia.org\&coprimary=primary\&titles=List+of+municipalities+in+Colorado\&section=Municipalities)

- [GPX (secondary coordinates)](https://geoexport.toolforge.org/gpx?project=en.wikipedia.org\&coprimary=secondary\&titles=List+of+municipalities+in+Colorado\&section=Municipalities)

Colorado has 272 [active incorporated municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_corporation), comprising 197 [towns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Town), 73 [cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City), and two [consolidated city and county governments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consolidated_city-county).[^cite_note-MuniIncCO-196][^cite_note-Colorado_incplace-205] At the [2020 United States census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_census), 4,299,942 of the 5,773,714 Colorado residents (74.47%) lived in one of these municipalities. Another 714,417 residents (12.37%) lived in one of the 210 [census-designated places](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_census-designated_places_in_Colorado), while the remaining 759,355 residents (13.15%) lived in the many rural and mountainous areas of the state.[^cite_note-Census-194]

Colorado's 272 municipalities operate under one of five types of [municipal governing authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_Colorado#Municipal_government). Colorado currently has two [consolidated city and county governments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_Colorado#Consolidated_city_and_county), 62 [home rule cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_Colorado#Home_rule_municipality), 41 [home rule towns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_Colorado#Home_rule_municipality), 11 [statutory cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_Colorado#Statutory_city), 155 [statutory towns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_Colorado#Statutory_town), and one [territorial charter municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_Colorado#Territorial_charter_municipality).

The most populous municipality is the [City and County of Denver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver). Colorado has 12 municipalities with more than 100,000 residents, and 17 with fewer than 100 residents. The 17 most populous Colorado municipalities are all located in the weather-protected [Front Range Urban Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Front_Range_Urban_Corridor). The [City of Grand Junction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Junction,_Colorado) is the most populous municipality on the [Colorado Western Slope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_Western_Slope). The [Town of Carbonate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbonate,_Colorado) has had no year-round population since the [1890 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1890_United_States_census) due to its severe winter weather and difficult access.[^cite_note-2024_muni_estimates-207]

[Highlands Ranch, Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highlands_Ranch,_Colorado).

In addition to its 272 municipalities, Colorado has 210 [unincorporated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unincorporated_area) [census-designated places (CDPs)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Census-designated_place) and many other small communities. The most populous unincorporated community in Colorado is [Highlands Ranch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highlands_Ranch,_Colorado) south of Denver. The seven most populous CDPs are located in the [Front Range Urban Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Front_Range_Urban_Corridor). The [Clifton CDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clifton,_Colorado) is the most populous CDP on the [Colorado Western Slope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_Western_Slope).[^cite_note-CO_CDP_2020-208]

### Special districts

Colorado has more than 4,000 [special districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Special_districts_in_Colorado), most with [property tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Property_tax) authority. These districts may provide [health services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Health_Service_Districts_in_Colorado), ambulance, schools, law enforcement, fire protection, water, sewage, drainage, irrigation, transportation, recreation, infrastructure, cultural facilities, business support, redevelopment, or other services.[^cite_note-209]



A [Regional Transportation District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_Transportation_District) A Line train at [Denver Union Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver_Union_Station).

Some of these districts have the authority to levy sales tax as well as property tax and use fees.

Some of the more notable Colorado districts are:

- The [Regional Transportation District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_Transportation_District) (RTD), which affects the counties of Denver, Boulder, Jefferson, and portions of Adams, Arapahoe, Broomfield, and Douglas Counties

- The [Scientific and Cultural Facilities District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scientific_and_Cultural_Facilities_District) (SCFD) that provides funding for cultural programing in Adams, Arapahoe, Boulder, Broomfield, Denver, Douglas, and Jefferson Counties

- The Football Stadium District (FD or FTBL), approved by the voters to pay for and help build the [Denver Broncos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver_Broncos)' stadium [Empower Field at Mile High](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empower_Field_at_Mile_High).

- Local Improvement Districts (LID) within designated areas of Jefferson and Broomfield counties.

- The Metropolitan Major League Baseball Stadium District, approved by voters to pay for and help build the [Colorado Rockies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_Rockies)' stadium [Coors Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coors_Field).

- Regional Transportation Authority (RTA) taxes at varying rates in Basalt, Carbondale, Glenwood Springs, and Gunnison County.

The 17 [core-based statistical areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Core-based_statistical_area) in the [U.S.](3434750.md) [State of Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Colorado).

Main article: [List of statistical areas in Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_statistical_areas_in_Colorado)

Most recently on July 21, 2023, the [Office of Management and Budget](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_Management_and_Budget) defined 21 statistical areas for Colorado comprising four [combined statistical areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combined_statistical_area), seven [metropolitan statistical areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_statistical_area), and ten [micropolitan statistical areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Micropolitan_statistical_area).[^cite_note-OMB-20-01-210]

The most populous of the seven [metropolitan statistical areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_statistical_area) in Colorado is the 10-county [Denver–Aurora–Centennial, CO Metropolitan Statistical Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver%E2%80%93Aurora%E2%80%93Centennial,_CO_Metropolitan_Statistical_Area) with a population of 2,963,821 at the [2020 United States census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_census), an increase of +15.29% since the [2010 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_United_States_census).[^cite_note-Census-194]

The more extensive 12-county [Denver–Aurora–Greeley, CO Combined Statistical Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver%E2%80%93Aurora%E2%80%93Greeley,_CO_Combined_Statistical_Area) had a population of 3,623,560 at the 2020 census, an increase of +17.23% since the 2010 census.[^cite_note-Census-194]

The most populous extended metropolitan region in Rocky Mountain Region is the 18-county [Front Range Urban Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Front_Range_Urban_Corridor) along the northeast face of the [Southern Rocky Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Rocky_Mountains). This region with Denver at its center had a population of 5,055,344 at the 2020 census, an increase of +16.65% since the 2010 census.[^cite_note-Census-194]

Largest Non-Hispanic Native American ancestry by county and numbers of people reporting "Native American Alone"

[Coloradan Hispanics and Latinos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanics_and_Latinos_in_Colorado) (of any race and heritage) made up 20.7% of the population.[^cite_note-218] According to the 2000 census, the largest ancestry groups in Colorado are [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_Americans) (22%), [Mexican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_Americans) (18%), [Irish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irish_Americans) (12%), and [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_Americans) (12%). Persons reporting German ancestry are especially numerous in the Front Range, the Rockies (west-central counties), and Eastern parts/High Plains.[^cite_note-219]

Colorado has a high proportion of [Hispanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanic), mostly [Mexican-American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican-American), citizens in Metropolitan Denver, Colorado Springs, as well as the smaller cities of Greeley and Pueblo, and elsewhere. Southern, Southwestern, and Southeastern Colorado have a large number of Hispanos, the descendants of the early settlers of colonial Spanish origin. In 1940, the U.S. Census Bureau reported Colorado's population as 8.2% Hispanic and 90.3% non-Hispanic White.[^cite_note-220] The Hispanic population of Colorado has continued to grow quickly over the past decades. By 2019, Hispanics made up 22% of Colorado's population, and [Non-Hispanic Whites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Hispanic_White) made up 70%.[^cite_note-quickfacts.census.gov-221] Spoken English in Colorado has many Spanish idioms.[^cite_note-222]

Colorado also has some large African-American communities located in Denver, in the neighborhoods of Montbello, Five Points, Whittier, and many other East Denver areas. In the [2020 Census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_Census), 234,828 Colorado residents were identified as African American (of the total 5,773,714).[^cite_note-223] [Arapahoe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arapahoe_County,_Colorado), at 10.8% African American, was the only one of the state's 64 counties where African Americans make up more than 10% of the population. African Americans in the seven counties of Arapahoe (70,787), [Denver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver_County,_Colorado) (64,038), [El Paso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Paso_County,_Colorado) (43,822), [Adams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adams_County,_Colorado) (17,463), [Jefferson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jefferson_County,_Colorado) (7,356), [Douglas](56310.md) (5,160), and [Weld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weld_County,_Colorado) (4,539) make up more than 90% of all African Americans in the state.[^cite_note-224]

The population of Native Americans in the state is small. Native Americans are concentrated in metropolitan Denver and the southwestern corner of Colorado, where there are two Ute reservations.[^cite_note-225]

The majority of Colorado's immigrants are from [Mexico](3966054.md), [India](14533.md), [China](5405.md), [Vietnam](202354.md), [Korea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korea), [Germany](11867.md) and [Canada](5042916.md).[^cite_note-226]

There were a total of 70,331 births in Colorado in 2006. (Birth rate of 14.6 per thousand.) In 2007, non-Hispanic Whites were involved in 59.1% of all births.[^cite_note-227] Some 14.06% of those births involved a non-Hispanic White person and someone of a different race, most often with a couple including one Hispanic. A birth where at least one Hispanic person was involved counted for 43% of the births in Colorado.[^cite_note-228] As of the [2010 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_United_States_census), Colorado has the seventh highest percentage of Hispanics (20.7%) in the U.S. behind [New Mexico](21649.md) (46.3%), California (37.6%), [Texas](29810.md) (37.6%), [Arizona](21883824.md) (29.6%), [Nevada](21216.md) (26.5%), and Florida (22.5%). Per the 2000 census, the Hispanic population is estimated to be 918,899, or approximately 20% of the state's total population. Colorado has the 5th-largest population of Mexican-Americans, behind California, Texas, Arizona, and [Illinois](14849.md). In percentages, Colorado has the 6th-highest percentage of Mexican-Americans, behind New Mexico, California, Texas, Arizona, and Nevada.[^cite_note-229]

Colorado [population pyramid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_pyramid)

In 2017, Colorado recorded the second-lowest fertility rate in the United States outside of New England, after [Oregon](26811621.md), at 1.63 children per woman.[^cite_note-autogenerated1-235] Significant contributing factors to the decline in pregnancies were the [ Title X Family Planning Program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Title_X) and an [intrauterine device](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intrauterine_device) grant from [Warren Buffett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warren_Buffett)'s family.[^cite_note-243][^cite_note-244]

### Language

See also: [Native American languages of Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_American_languages_of_Colorado)

English, the official language of the state, is the most commonly spoken language in Colorado.[^cite_note-\:5-245] The second most commonly spoken language in the state is Spanish.[^cite_note-\:5-245] The [Colorado River Numic language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_River_Numic_language), also known as the [Ute dialect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ute_dialect), is still spoken in Colorado.[^cite_note-246]

### Religion

Religious self-identification, per [Public Religion Research Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Religion_Research_Institute)'s 2022 *American Values Survey*

[^cite_note-247]

1. [Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism_in_the_United_States) 39 (38.2%)

2. [Catholicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholicism_in_the_United_States) 19 (18.6%)

3. [Mormonism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mormonism) 2 (1.96%)

4. [Eastern Orthodoxy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodoxy) 1 (0.98%)

5. [Unitarianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitarianism)/[Unitarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitarian_Universalism) 1 (0.98%)

6. [Judaism](15624.md) 1 (0.98%)

7. [New Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Age) 2 (1.96%)

8. East Asian Religions 2 (1.96%)

9. [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_the_United_States) 1 (0.98%)

10. [No religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irreligion_in_the_United_States) 34 (33.3%)

Major religious affiliations of the people of Colorado as of 2014 were 64% Christian, of whom there are 44% Protestant, 16% [Roman Catholic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholicism_in_the_United_States), 3% [Mormon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints), and 1% [Eastern Orthodox](10186.md).[^cite_note-248] Other religious breakdowns according to the Pew Research Center were 1% [Judaism](15624.md), 1% [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim), 1% [Buddhist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist), and 4% other. [Secular](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secularity) Coloradans made up 29% of the population.[^cite_note-249] In 2020, according to the Public Religion Research Institute, Christianity was 66% of the population. Judaism was also reported to have increased in this separate study, forming 2% of the religious landscape, while the religiously unaffiliated were reported to form 28% of the population in this separate study.[^cite_note-250] In 2022, the same organization reported 61% was Christian (39% Protestant, 19% Catholic, 2% [Mormon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mormons), 1% [Eastern Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Orthodoxy)), 2% [New Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Age), 1% Jewish, 1% Hindu, and 34% religiously unaffiliated.

According to the [Association of Religion Data Archives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_of_Religion_Data_Archives), the largest Christian denominations by the number of adherents in 2010 were the [Catholic Church](606848.md) with 811,630; multi-denominational Evangelical Protestants with 229,981; and [the Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints) with 151,433.[^cite_note-251] In 2020, the Association of Religion Data Archives determined the largest Christian denominations were Catholics (873,236), non/multi/inter-denominational Protestants (406,798), and Mormons (150,509).[^cite_note-252] Throughout its non-Christian population, there were 12,500 [Hindus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_the_United_States), 7,101 [Hindu Yogis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoga), and 17,369 [Buddhists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhism_in_the_United_States) at the 2020 study.

Our Lady of Guadalupe Catholic Church was the first permanent Catholic parish in modern-day Colorado and was constructed by Spanish colonists from New Mexico in modern-day [Conejos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conejos,_Colorado).[^cite_note-253] [Latin Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_Church) Catholics are served by three dioceses: the [Archdiocese of Denver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Denver) and the [Dioceses of Colorado Springs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Colorado_Springs) and [Pueblo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Pueblo).

The first permanent settlement by members of [the Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints in Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints_in_Colorado) arrived from Mississippi and initially camped along the Arkansas River just east of the present-day site of Pueblo.[^cite_note-254]

## Health

Colorado is generally considered among the healthiest states by behavioral and healthcare researchers. Among the positive contributing factors is the state's well-known outdoor recreation opportunities and initiatives.[^cite_note-255] However, there is a stratification of health metrics with wealthier counties such as [Douglas](56310.md) and [Pitkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pitkin_County,_Colorado) performing significantly better relative to southern, less wealthy counties such as [Huerfano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huerfano_County,_Colorado) and [Las Animas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Las_Animas_County,_Colorado).[^cite_note-\:0-17]

### Obesity

According to several studies, Coloradans have the lowest rates of obesity of any state in the US.[^cite_note-256] As of 2018, 24% of the population was considered medically obese, and while the lowest in the nation, the percentage had increased from 17% in 2004.[^cite_note-257][^cite_note-258]

### Life expectancy

According to a report in the *[Journal of the American Medical Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Journal_of_the_American_Medical_Association)*, residents of Colorado had a 2014 [life expectancy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Life_expectancy) of 80.21 years, the longest of any U.S. state.[^cite_note-259]

### Homelessness

According to [HUD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Housing_and_Urban_Development)'s 2022 [Annual Homeless Assessment Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annual_Homeless_Assessment_Report_to_Congress), there were an estimated 10,397 [homeless people in Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homelessness_in_Colorado).[^cite_note-260][^cite_note-261]

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Colorado)



Denver Energy Center lies in the Denver financial district along 17th Street, known as the "[Wall Street of the West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wall_Street_of_the_West)"



[Corn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maize) growing in [Larimer County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larimer_County,_Colorado)

In 2019 the total employment was 2,473,192. The number of employer establishments is 174,258.[^cite_note-262]

Colorado's [gross domestic product](12594.md) in 2025 was \$584.3 billion.[^cite_note-263] Colorado's [per capita personal income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_by_GDP_per_capita_\(nominal\)) was \$86,181 in 2025, ranking Colorado 10th in the nation.[^cite_note-264] Median Annual Household Income in 2016 was \$70,666, 8th in the nation.[^cite_note-265] The state's economy broadened from its mid-19th-century roots in mining when irrigated agriculture developed, and by the late 19th century, raising livestock had become important. Early industry was based on the extraction and processing of minerals and agricultural products. Current agricultural products are cattle, wheat, [dairy products](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dairy_product), [corn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maize), and [hay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hay). In 2025, [small businesses](276495.md) made up 99.5% of Colorado businesses, and employed 48.6% of the state's work force.[^cite_note-266]

The [federal government](195149.md) operates several federal facilities in the state, including [NORAD](42905.md) (North American Aerospace Defense Command), [United States Air Force Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Air_Force_Academy), Schriever Air Force Base located approximately 10 miles (16 kilometers) east of [Peterson Air Force Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peterson_Air_Force_Base), and [Fort Carson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Carson), both located in [Colorado Springs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_Springs,_Colorado) within El Paso County; [NOAA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NOAA), the National Renewable Energy Laboratory ([NREL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NREL)) in Golden, and the [National Institute of Standards and Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institute_of_Standards_and_Technology) in [Boulder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boulder,_Colorado); [U.S. Geological Survey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Geological_Survey) and other government agencies at the [Denver Federal Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver_Federal_Center) near [Lakewood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lakewood,_Colorado); the [Denver Mint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver_Mint), [Buckley Space Force Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buckley_Space_Force_Base), the [Tenth Circuit Court of Appeals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Court_of_Appeals_for_the_Tenth_Circuit), and the [Byron G. Rogers Federal Building and United States Courthouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byron_G._Rogers_Federal_Building_and_United_States_Courthouse) in Denver; and a federal [Supermax Prison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ADX_Florence) and other federal prisons near [Cañon City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ca%C3%B1on_City,_Colorado). In addition to these and other [federal agencies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_Agencies_of_the_United_States_Government), Colorado has abundant [National Forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_National_Forest) land and four [National Parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_National_Parks_of_the_United_States) that contribute to federal ownership of 24,615,788 acres (99,617 km<sup>2</sup>) of land in Colorado, or 37% of the total area of the state.[^cite_note-267]

In the second half of the 20th century, the industrial and service sectors expanded greatly. The state's economy is diversified and is notable for its concentration on scientific research and high-technology industries. Other industries include [food processing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_processing), transportation equipment, [machinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mechanical_engineering), [chemical products](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chemical_engineering), the extraction of metals such as gold (see [Gold mining in Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gold_mining_in_Colorado)), silver, and [molybdenum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molybdenum). Colorado now also has the largest annual production of beer in any state.[^cite_note-268] Denver is an important financial center.

The state's diverse [geography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Colorado) and majestic mountains attract millions of tourists every year, including 85.2 million in 2018. Tourism contributes greatly to Colorado's economy, with tourists generating \$22.3 billion in 2018.[^cite_note-269]

Several nationally known brand names have originated in Colorado factories and laboratories. From [Denver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver#Economy) came the forerunner of telecommunications giant [Qwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qwest_Corporation) in 1879, [Samsonite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samsonite) luggage in 1910, [Gates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gates_Corporation) belts and hoses in 1911, and [Russell Stover Candies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russell_Stover_Candies) in 1923. Kuner canned vegetables began in [Brighton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brighton,_Colorado) in 1864. From [Golden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden,_Colorado) came [Coors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coors_Brewing_Company) beer in 1873, [CoorsTek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CoorsTek) industrial ceramics in 1920, and [Jolly Rancher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jolly_Rancher) candy in 1949. [CF\&I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_Fuel_and_Iron) railroad rails, wire, nails, and pipe debuted in [Pueblo](24158.md) in 1892. [Holly Sugar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holly_Sugar) was first milled from beets in [Holly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holly,_Colorado) in 1905, and later moved its headquarters to Colorado Springs. The present-day [Swift](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swift_&_Company) packed meat of [Greeley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greeley,_Colorado) evolved from Monfort of Colorado, Inc., established in 1930. [Estes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estes_Industries) model rockets were launched in [Penrose](108498.md) in 1958. [Fort Collins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Collins,_Colorado) has been the home of [Woodward Governor Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woodward_Governor_Company)'s motor controllers (governors) since 1870, and [Waterpik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oral_irrigator) dental water jets and showerheads since 1962. [Celestial Seasonings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celestial_Seasonings) herbal teas have been made in [Boulder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boulder,_Colorado) since 1969. [Rocky Mountain Chocolate Factory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocky_Mountain_Chocolate_Factory) made its first candy in [Durango](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Durango,_Colorado) in 1981.

Colorado has a flat 4.63% [income tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Income_tax), regardless of income level. On November 3, 2020, voters authorized an initiative to lower that income tax rate to 4.55 percent. Unlike most states, which calculate taxes based on federal *adjusted gross income*, Colorado taxes are based on *taxable income*—income after federal exemptions and federal itemized (or standard) deductions.[^cite_note-270][^cite_note-271] Colorado's state [sales tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sales_tax) is 2.9% on retail sales. When state revenues exceed state constitutional limits, according to Colorado's [Taxpayer Bill of Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxpayer_Bill_of_Rights) legislation, full-year Colorado residents can claim a sales tax refund on their individual state income tax return. Many counties and cities charge their own rates, in addition to the base state rate. There are also certain county and special district taxes that may apply.

Real estate and personal business property are taxable in Colorado. The state's senior property tax exemption was temporarily suspended by the Colorado Legislature in 2003. The tax break was scheduled to return for the assessment year 2006, payable in 2007.[^cite_note-272]

As of May 2025, the state's unemployment rate was 4.8%.[^cite_note-273]

The [West Virginia teachers' strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_West_Virginia_teachers'_strike) in 2018 inspired [teachers in other states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018%E2%80%9319_education_workers'_strikes_in_the_United_States), including [Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Colorado_teachers'_strike), to take similar action.[^cite_note-274]

### Agriculture

[Corn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corn) is grown in the [Eastern Plains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Plains) of Colorado. Arid conditions and drought negatively impacted yields in 2020 and 2022.[^cite_note-275]

### Natural resources



An [oil well](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_well) in western Colorado

Colorado has significant [hydrocarbon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydrocarbon) resources. According to the [Energy Information Administration](1704673.md), Colorado hosts seven of the largest natural gas fields in the United States, and two of the largest [oil fields](2910801.md). Conventional and unconventional natural gas output from several Colorado basins typically accounts for more than five percent of annual U.S. natural gas production. Colorado's [oil shale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_shale) deposits hold an estimated 1 trillion barrels (160 km<sup>3</sup>) of oil—nearly as much oil as the entire world's proven oil reserves.[^cite_note-276] Substantial deposits of [bituminous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bituminous_coal), [subbituminous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sub-bituminous_coal), and [lignite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lignite) coal are found in the state.[^cite_note-277]

[Uranium mining in Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uranium_mining_in_Colorado) goes back to 1872, when pitchblende ore was taken from gold mines near Central City, Colorado. Not counting byproduct uranium from [phosphate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phosphate), Colorado is considered to have the third-largest uranium reserves of any U.S. state, behind Wyoming and New Mexico. When Colorado and Utah dominated [radium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radium) mining from 1910 to 1922, uranium and [vanadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanadium) were the byproducts (giving towns like present-day [Superfund site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Superfund_site) [Uravan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uravan,_Colorado) their names).[^cite_note-278] Uranium price increases from 2001 to 2007 prompted several companies to revive uranium mining in Colorado. During the 1940s certain communities–including [Naturita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naturita,_Colorado) and [Paradox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paradox,_Colorado)–earned the moniker of "[yellowcake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yellowcake) towns" from their relationship with uranium mining. Price drops and financing problems in late 2008 forced these companies to cancel or scale back the uranium-mining project. As of 2016, there were no major uranium mining operations in the state, though plans existed to restart production.[^cite_note-279]

### Electricity generation

See also: [List of power stations in Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_power_stations_in_Colorado)

Colorado's high Rocky Mountain ridges and eastern plains offer [wind power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wind_power_in_Colorado) potential, and geologic activity in the mountain areas provides the potential for [geothermal power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geothermal_energy_in_the_United_States) development. Much of the state is sunny and could produce [solar power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solar_power_in_Colorado). Major rivers flowing from the Rocky Mountains offer [hydroelectric power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_dams_and_reservoirs_in_Colorado) resources.[^cite_note-280]

## Culture



[History Colorado Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_Colorado_Center) in Denver

### Arts and film

- [List of museums in Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_museums_in_Colorado)

- [List of theaters in Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_theaters_in_Colorado)

- [Music of Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Colorado)

Several film productions have been shot on location in Colorado, especially prominent [Westerns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_\(genre\)) like *[True Grit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/True_Grit_\(1969_film\))*, *[The Searchers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Searchers_\(film\))*, *[City Slickers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Slickers),* *[Butch Cassidy and the Sundance Kid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Butch_Cassidy_and_the_Sundance_Kid), and [My Life With the Walter Boys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/My_Life_with_the_Walter_Boys)*. Several historic military forts, railways with trains still operating, and mining [ghost towns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_ghost_towns_in_Colorado) have been used and transformed for historical accuracy in well-known films. There are also several scenic highways and mountain passes that helped to feature the open road in films such as *[Vanishing Point](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanishing_Point_\(1971_film\))*, *[Bingo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bingo_\(1991_film\))* and *[Starman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starman_\(1984_film\))*. Some Colorado landmarks have been featured in films, such as [The Stanley Hotel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Stanley_Hotel) in *[Dumb and Dumber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dumb_and_Dumber)* and *[The Shining](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Shining_\(miniseries\))* and the [Sculptured House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sculptured_House) in *[Sleeper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sleeper_\(1973_film\))*. In 2015, *[Furious 7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Furious_7)* was to film driving sequences on [Pikes Peak Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pikes_Peak_Highway) in Colorado. The TV adult-animated series *[South Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Park)* takes place in central Colorado in the titular town. Additionally, the TV series *[Good Luck Charlie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Good_Luck_Charlie)* was set, but not filmed, in Denver, Colorado.[^cite_note-281] The Colorado Office of Film and Television has noted that more than 400 films have been shot in Colorado.[^cite_note-282]

There are also several established film festivals in Colorado, including [Aspen Filmfest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aspen_Filmfest) and [Aspen Shortsfest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aspen_Shortsfest), [Boulder International Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boulder_International_Film_Festival), [Castle Rock Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Castle_Rock_Film_Festival), [Denver Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver_Film_Festival), [Festivus Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Festivus_Film_Festival), [Mile High Horror Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mile_High_Horror_Film_Festival), [Moondance International Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moondance_International_Film_Festival), [Mountainfilm in Telluride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountainfilm_in_Telluride), [Rocky Mountain Women's Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocky_Mountain_Women's_Film_Festival), and [Telluride Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telluride_Film_Festival). On March 27, 2025, it was announced [Sundance Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sundance_Film_Festival) would move to Boulder starting in 2027 after reaching a deal for a ten-year duration.[^cite_note-283]

Many notable writers have lived or spent extended periods in Colorado. *[5280](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5280)*, a Denver magazine, wrote in 2015 that [Kent Haruf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kent_Haruf) is "widely considered \[to be] Colorado's finest novelist"; Haruf set his novels in the fictional high plains Colorado town of Holt.[^cite_note-\:8-284] [Beat Generation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beat_Generation) writers [Jack Kerouac](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Kerouac) and [Neal Cassady](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neal_Cassady) lived in and around Denver for several years each.[^cite_note-285] Irish playwright [Oscar Wilde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscar_Wilde) visited Colorado on his tour of the United States in 1882, writing in his 1906 *Impressions of America* that [Leadville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leadville,_Colorado) was "the richest city in the world. It has also got the reputation of being the roughest, and every man carries a [revolver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolver)."[^cite_note-286][^cite_note-287]

### Cuisine

Colorado is known for its [Southwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuisine_of_the_Southwestern_United_States) and [Rocky Mountain cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocky_Mountain_cuisine), with Mexican restaurants found throughout the state.[^cite_note-288][^cite_note-289]

[Boulder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boulder,_Colorado) was named America's Foodiest Town 2010 by [Bon Appétit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bon_App%C3%A9tit).[^cite_note-Bon_Appétit-290] Boulder, and Colorado in general, is home to several national food and beverage companies, top-tier restaurants and farmers' markets. Boulder also has more Master [Sommeliers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sommelier) per capita than any other city, including San Francisco and New York.[^cite_note-Denver_Magazine-291] [Denver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver) is known for steak, but now has a diverse culinary scene with many restaurants.[^cite_note-Travel_+_Leisure-292]

[Polidori Sausage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polidori_Sausage) is a brand of pork products available in supermarkets, which originated in Colorado, in the early 20th century.[^cite_note-293]

The [Food & Wine Classic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_&_Wine_Classic) is held annually each June in [Aspen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aspen,_Colorado). Aspen also has a reputation as the culinary capital of the Rocky Mountain region.[^cite_note-294]

### Wine and beer

Main articles: [Colorado wine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_wine) and [Colorado beer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_beer)

[Colorado wines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_wine) include varietals that have attracted favorable notice from outside the state.[^cite_note-295] With wines made from traditional *[Vitis vinifera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vitis_vinifera)* grapes along with wines made from cherries, peaches, plums, and honey, Colorado wines have won top national and international awards for their quality.[^cite_note-Jefferson_Cup_Invitational_Wine_Competition-296] Colorado's grape growing regions contain the highest elevation [vineyards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vineyard) in the United States,[^cite_note-297] with most [viticulture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viticulture) in the state practiced between 4,000 and 7,000 feet (1,219 and 2,134 m) [above sea level](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Above_sea_level). The mountain [climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate) ensures warm summer days and cool nights. Colorado is home to two designated [American Viticultural Areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Viticultural_Area) of the [Grand Valley AVA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Valley_AVA) and the [West Elks AVA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Elks_AVA),[^cite_note-Alcohol_and_Tobacco_Tax_and_Trade_Bureau-298] where most of the vineyards in the state are located. However, an increasing number of wineries are located along the [Front Range](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Front_Range).[^cite_note-Colorado_Wine_Industry_Development_Board-299] In 2018, [Wine Enthusiast Magazine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wine_Enthusiast_Magazine) named Colorado's Grand Valley AVA in Mesa County, Colorado, as one of the Top Ten wine travel destinations in the world.[^cite_note-300]

Colorado is home to many nationally praised [microbreweries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microbreweries),[^cite_note-301] including [New Belgium Brewing Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Belgium_Brewing_Company), [Odell Brewing Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odell_Brewing_Company), and [Great Divide Brewing Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Divide_Brewing_Company). The area of northern Colorado near and between the cities of Denver, [Boulder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boulder,_Colorado), and [Fort Collins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Collins) is known as the "Napa Valley of Beer" due to its high density of [craft breweries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Craft_breweries).[^cite_note-The_Denver_Beer_Triangle-302]

### Marijuana and hemp

Colorado is open to [cannabis](1481886.md) (marijuana) [tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recreational_drug_use).[^cite_note-303] With the adoption of the [64th state amendment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_Amendment_64) in 2012, Colorado became the first state in the union to [legalize marijuana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legality_of_cannabis_by_U.S._jurisdiction) for [medicinal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_cannabis_in_the_United_States) (2000), industrial (referring to [hemp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hemp), 2012), and [recreational](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Colorado_Amendment_64) (2012) use. Colorado's marijuana industry sold \$1.31 billion worth of marijuana in 2016 and \$1.26 billion in the first three-quarters of 2017.[^cite_note-304] The state generated tax, fee, and license revenue of \$194 million in 2016 on legal marijuana sales.[^cite_note-305] Colorado regulates hemp as any part of the plant with less than 0.3% THC.[^cite_note-306]

On April 4, 2014, Senate Bill 14–184 addressing oversight of Colorado's industrial hemp program was first introduced, ultimately being signed into law by Governor John Hickenlooper on May 31, 2014.[^cite_note-307]

#### Medicinal use

On November 7, 2000, 54% of Colorado voters passed Amendment 20, which amends the Colorado State constitution to allow the [medical use of marijuana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_use_of_cannabis).[^cite_note-norml-co-308] A patient's medical use of marijuana, within the following limits, is lawful:

- (I) No more than 2 ounces (57 g) of a usable form of marijuana; and

- (II) No more than twelve marijuana plants, with six or fewer being mature, flowering plants that are producing a usable form of marijuana.

  [^cite_note-309]

Currently, Colorado has listed "eight medical conditions for which patients can use marijuana—cancer, glaucoma, HIV/AIDS, muscle spasms, seizures, severe pain, severe nausea and cachexia, or dramatic weight loss and muscle atrophy".[^cite_note-Young-310] While governor, [John Hickenlooper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Hickenlooper) allocated about half of the state's \$13 million "Medical Marijuana Program Cash Fund"[^cite_note-MMPCF-311] to medical research in the 2014 budget.[^cite_note-Markus-312] By 2018, the Medical Marijuana Program Cash Fund was the "largest pool of pot money in the state" and was used to fund programs including research into pediatric applications for controlling autism symptoms.[^cite_note-313]

#### Recreational use

On November 6, 2012, voters amended the state constitution to protect "personal use" of marijuana for adults, establishing a framework to regulate marijuana in a manner similar to [alcohol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alcohol_\(drug\)).[^cite_note-314] The first recreational marijuana shops in Colorado, and by extension the United States, opened their doors on January 1, 2014.[^cite_note-Healy-188]

## Transportation

Main article: [Transportation in Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transportation_in_Colorado)



A Colorado state welcome sign

Colorado's primary mode of transportation (in terms of passengers) is its highway system. [Interstate 25](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_25_in_Colorado) (I-25) is the primary north–south highway in the state, connecting Pueblo, Colorado Springs, Denver, and Fort Collins, and extending north to Wyoming and south to New Mexico. [I-70](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_70_in_Colorado) is the primary east–west corridor. It connects Grand Junction and the mountain communities with Denver and enters Utah and Kansas. The state is home to a network of US and Colorado highways that provide access to all principal areas of the state. Many smaller communities are connected to this network only via county roads.



The main terminal of Denver International Airport evokes the peaks of the [Front Range](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Front_Range).

[Denver International Airport](263648.md) (DIA) is the third-busiest domestic U.S. and [international airport in the world by passenger traffic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World's_busiest_airports_by_passenger_traffic).[^cite_note-315] DIA handles by far the largest volume of commercial air traffic in Colorado and is the busiest U.S. hub airport between Chicago and the Pacific coast, making Denver the most important airport for connecting passenger traffic in the western United States.

Public transportation bus services are offered both intra-city and inter-city—including the Denver metro area's RTD services. The [Regional Transportation District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_Transportation_District) (RTD) operates the popular [RTD Bus & Rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RTD_Bus_&_Rail) transit system in the [Denver Metropolitan Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver_Metropolitan_Area). As of January 2013 the RTD rail system had 170 light-rail vehicles, serving 47 miles (76 km) of track. In addition to local public transit, intercity bus service is provided by [Burlington Trailways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burlington_Trailways), [Bustang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bustang) and [Greyhound Lines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greyhound_Lines).



The westbound and eastbound *[California Zephyrs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Zephyr)* meet in the [Glenwood Canyon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glenwood_Canyon).

[Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak) operates two passenger rail lines in Colorado, the *[California Zephyr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Zephyr)* and *[Southwest Chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southwest_Chief)*. Colorado's contribution to world railroad history was forged principally by the [Denver and Rio Grande Western Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver_and_Rio_Grande_Western_Railroad) which began in 1870 and wrote the book on mountain railroading. In 1988 the "Rio Grande" was acquired, but was merged into, the [Southern Pacific Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Pacific_Railroad) by their joint owner [Philip Anschutz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philip_Anschutz). On September 11, 1996, Anschutz sold the combined company to the [Union Pacific Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Pacific_Railroad), creating the largest railroad network in the United States. The Anschutz sale was partly in response to the earlier merger of Burlington Northern and Santa Fe which formed the large Burlington Northern and Santa Fe Railway (BNSF), Union Pacific's principal competitor in western U.S. railroading. Both Union Pacific and BNSF have extensive freight operations in Colorado.

Colorado's freight railroad network consists of 2,688 miles of Class I trackage. It is integral to the U.S. economy, being a critical artery for the movement of energy, agriculture, mining, and industrial commodities as well as general freight and manufactured products between the East and Midwest and the Pacific coast states.[^cite_note-316]

In August 2014, Colorado began to issue driver licenses to [aliens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alien_\(law\)) [not lawfully in the United States](5044573.md) who lived in Colorado.[^cite_note-317] In September 2014, [KCNC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KCNC) reported that 524 non-citizens were issued Colorado driver licenses that are normally issued to U.S. citizens living in Colorado.[^cite_note-318]

## Education

Main articles: [List of colleges and universities in Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_colleges_and_universities_in_Colorado) and [List of high schools in Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_high_schools_in_Colorado)

See also: [Table of Colorado school districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_of_Colorado_school_districts), [Table of Colorado charter schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_of_Colorado_charter_schools), and [Auraria Campus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auraria_Campus)

The first institution of [higher education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Higher_education_in_the_United_States) in the Colorado Territory was the [Colorado Seminary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_Seminary), opened on November 16, 1864, by the [Methodist Episcopal Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Methodist_Episcopal_Church). The seminary closed in 1867 but reopened in 1880 as the [University of Denver](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Denver). In 1870, the Bishop [George Maxwell Randall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_M._Randall_\(bishop\)) of the [Episcopal Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Episcopal_Church_\(United_States\))'s [Missionary District of Colorado and Parts Adjacent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Episcopal_Diocese_of_Colorado) opened the first of what become the [Colorado University Schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_University_Schools) which would include the Territorial School of Mines opened in 1873 and sold to the Colorado Territory in 1874. These schools were initially run by the Episcopal Church.[^cite_note-319] An 1861 territorial act called for the creation of a public university in Boulder, though it would not be until 1876 that the [University of Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Colorado_Boulder) was founded.[^cite_note-320] The 1876 act also renamed Territorial School of Mines as the [Colorado School of Mines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_School_of_Mines).[^cite_note-321] An 1870 territorial act created the Agricultural College of Colorado which opened in 1879.[^cite_note-322] The college was renamed the Colorado State College of Agriculture and Mechanic Arts in 1935, and became [Colorado State University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_State_University) in 1957.[^cite_note-323]

The first [Catholic college](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Catholic_universities_and_colleges_in_the_United_States) in Colorado was the [Jesuit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Society_of_Jesus) Sacred Heart College, which was founded in New Mexico in 1877, moved to [Morrison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morrison,_Colorado) in 1884, and to Denver in 1887. The college was renamed Regis College in 1921 and [Regis University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regis_University) in 1991.[^cite_note-324] On April 1, 1924, armed students patrolled the campus after a [burning cross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cross_burning) was found, the climax of tensions between Regis College and the locally-powerful Ku Klux Klan.[^cite_note-325]

Following a 1950 assessment by the Service Academy Board, it was determined that there was a need to supplement the [U.S. Military](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Military_Academy) and [Naval Academies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Naval_Academy) with a third school that would provide commissioned officers for the newly independent Air Force. On April 1, 1954, President [Dwight Eisenhower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dwight_Eisenhower) signed a law that moved for the creation of a [U.S. Air Force Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Air_Force_Academy).[^cite_note-326] Later that year, Colorado Springs was selected to host the new institution. From its establishment in 1955, until the construction of appropriate facilities in Colorado Springs was completed and opened in 1958, the Air Force Academy operated out of [Lowry Air Force Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lowry_Air_Force_Base) in Denver. With the opening of the Colorado Springs facility, the cadets moved to the new campus, though not in the full-kit march that some urban and campus legends suggest.[^cite_note-327] The first class of [Space Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Space_Force) [officers](36301328.md) from the Air Force Academy commissioned on April 18, 2020.[^cite_note-328]

## Indigenous people



Map of Indian reservations in Colorado.



The ruins of the [Cliff Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cliff_Palace) of [Mesa Verde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesa_Verde_National_Park), photographed by [Gustaf Nordenskiöld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gustaf_Nordenski%C3%B6ld) in 1891

*See [Indigenous People of Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:Indigenous_People_of_CO)*

The two [Native American reservations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_reservation) remaining in Colorado are the [Southern Ute Indian Reservation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Ute_Indian_Reservation) (1873; [Ute dialect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ute_dialect): *Kapuuta-wa Moghwachi Núuchi-u*) and [Ute Mountain Ute Indian Reservation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ute_Mountain_Ute_Indian_Reservation) (1940; Ute dialect: *Wʉgama Núuchi*).[^cite_note-329]

The two terminated Indian reservations in Colorado were the [Cheyenne and Arapaho Indian Reservation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheyenne_and_Arapaho_Tribes) (1851–1870) and [Ute Indian Reservation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uintah_and_Ouray_Indian_Reservation) (1855–1873).[^cite_note-330]

## Sports



The [Colorado Rockies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_Rockies) baseball club at [Coors Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coors_Field)



[Empower Field at Mile High](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empower_Field_at_Mile_High) in Denver, home field of the [Denver Broncos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver_Broncos) and the [Denver Outlaws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver_Outlaws)



[Ball Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ball_Arena), home of the [Denver Nuggets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver_Nuggets), the [Colorado Avalanche](70948.md), and the [Colorado Mammoth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_Mammoth)



[Dick's Sporting Goods Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dick's_Sporting_Goods_Park), home of the [Colorado Rapids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_Rapids)



[Weidner Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weidner_Field) in Colorado Springs, home of the [Colorado Springs Switchbacks FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_Springs_Switchbacks_FC)

Main article: [Sports in Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_Colorado)

Colorado has five [major professional sports leagues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_professional_sports_leagues_in_the_United_States_and_Canada), all based in the Denver metropolitan area. Colorado is the least populous state with a franchise in each of the major professional sports leagues.

The [Pikes Peak International Hill Climb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pikes_Peak_International_Hill_Climb) is a major hill climbing motor race held on the Pikes Peak Highway.

The [Cherry Hills Country Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cherry_Hills_Country_Club) has hosted several professional golf tournaments, including the [U.S. Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Open_\(golf\)),[^cite_note-332] [U.S. Senior Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Senior_Open), [U.S. Women's Open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Women's_Open),[^cite_note-333] [PGA Championship](224616.md) and [BMW Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMW_Championship_\(PGA_Tour\)).[^cite_note-334]

| [Northern Colorado Bears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Colorado_Bears) | [University of Northern Colorado](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Northern_Colorado) | [Greeley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greeley,_Colorado) | [Big Sky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Sky_Conference)[^cite_note-339] |
| [Colorado College Tigers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_College_Tigers) | [Colorado College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_College) | [Colorado Springs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colorado_Springs,_Colorado) | [NCHC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Collegiate_Hockey_Conference) / [Mountain West](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_West_Conference)[^cite_note-340] |

[^cite_note-336]: &#x20;Several Air Force teams participate in other conferences, or as independents, in sports that the MW does not sponsor: Boxing, a men-only sport that is not sanctioned by the NCAA, competes as an independent. Fencing, a coeducational sport with men's and women's squads, also competes as an independent. Men's and women's gymnastics both compete in the [Mountain Pacific Sports Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_Pacific_Sports_Federation). Men's ice hockey competes in [Atlantic Hockey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Hockey). Men's lacrosse competes in the [ASUN Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ASUN_Conference). Rifle, which at Air Force is a coeducational sport, competes in the [Patriot Rifle Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriot_Rifle_Conference). Men's soccer and women's swimming & diving compete in the [Western Athletic Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Athletic_Conference). Men's wrestling competes in the [Big 12 Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_12_Conference).

[^cite_note-337]: &#x20;Several Colorado teams participate in other conferences in sports that the Big 12 does not sponsor: Men's and women's indoor track & field compete in the [Mountain Pacific Sports Federation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mountain_Pacific_Sports_Federation). Skiing, a coeducational sport with men's and women's squads, competes in the [Rocky Mountain Intercollegiate Ski Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocky_Mountain_Intercollegiate_Ski_Association).

[^cite_note-338]: &#x20;Several Denver teams participate in other conferences in sports that The Summit League does not sponsor: Women's gymnastics competes in the [Big 12 Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_12_Conference). Men's ice hockey competes in the [National Collegiate Hockey Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Collegiate_Hockey_Conference). Men's and women's lacrosse compete in the [Big East Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_East_Conference). Skiing, a coeducational sport with men's and women's squads, competes in the [Rocky Mountain Intercollegiate Ski Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rocky_Mountain_Intercollegiate_Ski_Association).

[^cite_note-339]: &#x20;Several Northern Colorado teams participate in other conferences in sports that the Big Sky does not sponsor: Baseball competes in the [Summit League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summit_League). Women's swimming & diving competes in the [Western Athletic Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Athletic_Conference). Men's wrestling competes in the [Big 12 Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_12_Conference).

[^cite_note-340]: &#x20;Colorado College, otherwise an [NCAA Division III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NCAA_Division_III) member, has two Division I teams. Men's ice hockey competes in the National Collegiate Hockey Conference and women's soccer competes in the Mountain West.
